The Los Angeles Rams dug themselves out of a 2-4 hole en route to 6-4.

They put together a four-game win streak. On the surface it seems like their early season struggles have all been corrected. In reality, their last four opponents have a combined record of 11-26-1.

It's reasonable to look at the last month's schedule and conclude we really learned nothing about the Rams. Of course, they should be given credit for keeping losing teams losing.

Who are the Rams following their recent rash of injuries? What happens when stiffer competition slows LA's run game and they must rely on an inconsistent and short-handed passing attack? The defense has stymied offenses of late. What happens when they face the likes of Jordan Love, Patrick Mahomes, Brock Purdy, and Dak Prescott in the coming weeks?

One thing is still true: the Rams have three lives left in the NFC playoff picture

It will take 10 wins to make the playoffs. In other words, they cannot take more than three losses moving forward without leaving their destiny up to factors outside of their control.

When looking on the horizon, LA's season effectively starts out of their bye week.

Perhaps the single-most important game left. The Rams are competing directly with NFC West teams in the race for their own division, although the 49ers may be running away with it. They are also indirectly competing against the NFC North. This Wednesday night game is the perfect opportunity to ding Green Bay and bolster their own chances. It carries significant playoff weight.

Unimportant in terms of the NFC tiebreakers, although the Rams really cannot afford to use one of their remaining three lives this early.

Can LA avenge their Week 1 loss where they looked enitely unprepared to play.

Dallas can light up the scoreboard. It will be a tough test for the defense. The offense may need more production than simply grounding the game out on the ground in order to keep pace.

It's tough closing the season out with the Seahawks in two of the final three weeks. The silver lining is that winning both matchups would effectively eliminate Seattle and reinforce LA's destiny. They must take care of business or they could be left on the outside looking in.

Real Estate Crowdfunding Starts at $10 – Your Money Can Be Stuck for Months

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Real estate crowdfunding lets you buy into property funds for as little as $10. Selling usually means waiting for the fund to buy your shares back, and funds cap those buybacks each quarter and sometimes halt them. Invest only money you can leave alone for five years, and keep it a small part of your portfolio.

Fundrise’s Equity REIT, a real estate investment trust, took no buyback requests from July 2025 until it merged into a larger fund, the Fundrise eREIT, in April 2026. RealtyMogul’s Income REIT and Apartment Growth REIT suspended buybacks that April and still weren’t taking requests in September.

Say you put $5,000 into the Fundrise eREIT and need it back a year later. Its quarterly report through September says it limits buybacks to 2.5% of its value a quarter, or up to 5% at its manager’s choice. Since the merger, investors had asked it to buy back about 17.9 million shares, and about 14 million of those shares were still waiting. Its prospectus also sets a 1% penalty on shares sold back within five years, $50 on a stake still worth $5,000.

Anyone can buy what these platforms sell to the public. Private deals usually take only accredited investors. The financial tests are income over $200,000 in each of the last two years and expected this year ($300,000 with a spouse or partner), or a net worth over $1 million not counting your home. Fees run about 1% a year. Fundrise charges a 0.85% management fee on its real estate funds plus a 0.15% advisory fee, and Arrived, which sells shares of single rental homes, builds its one-time fee for finding each house into the share price.

Hold most of your real estate money in a REIT index fund instead. Vanguard’s Real Estate ETF charges 0.13% a year against Fundrise’s 1% in fees and can be sold any trading day, though its price changes daily.

Before you invest, look up the fund on the SEC’s EDGAR site and open its latest annual report. Check it for suspended buybacks, and read the auditor’s report for a going-concern warning, which means the auditor doubts the company can keep operating for another year. Arrived Homes, which holds the houses behind Arrived’s single-home shares, got one for 2025 because it was short of cash.

Pass on any offering whose latest report shows either, however low its minimum.

Air New Zealand Launches New 12-Hour Nonstop Route In 1 Week: See Map Now

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Journalist – Aaron is an aviation and travel journalist with 14 years of experience in the travel and tourism industry. His background spans airport operations, aviation infrastructure, and travel consulting, having worked with global organizations across New Zealand, Europe, and North America. Combining industry expertise with firsthand travel experience across more than 52 countries and five continents, Aaron covers airlines, airports, aviation policy, and the wider travel sector. He is based in Canterbury, New Zealand. His work has been published in Simple Flying and featured by MSN, SAM CHUI Aviation & Travel, EIN News, Pakistan Defence, and Climate Depot.

Star Alliance carrier Air New Zealand is excited to expand its South Island long-haul network, with the airline already having introduced service from Christchurch (CHC) to Singapore (SIN) in October. This November, the airline will expand further, with the addition of Tokyo Narita (NRT) and Perth (PER) on a seasonal basis.

Historically, the airline has served these routes before. However, they were ultimately canceled, with services to Japan cut in 2015 from the Garden City, and Perth was shelved in 2019.

Air New Zealand will resume its nonstop service to Japan's largest city, Tokyo, on November 28. The route will be served with the airline's Boeing 787-9 aircraft, which is equipped with three classes of service: business, premium economy, and economy. The airline's Skycouch is also available on these flights, allowing passengers to book a full row to themselves, with the seats converting to a small bed, including a mattress topper, duvet, and pillows.

The flights to Tokyo will operate three times a week, with an afternoon departure from Christchurch, for a late evening arrival into Narita. Return flights will depart from Tokyo in the evening for an overnight flight south back to New Zealand's South Island.

For South Island travelers, the opportunity to now fly on this long-haul international route from Christchurch saves the need to transit via the country's busiest airport, Auckland (AKL), where the majority of all long-haul Air New Zealand services operate. Tokyo Narita will join Singapore (SIN) as the only two long-haul destinations by Air New Zealand from Christchurch, for just a couple of days; read more about that below.

Just two days after Air New Zealand adds Tokyo Narita, the airline will also resume its service to Perth (PER) in Western Australia. The airline already serves Perth year-round from Auckland; however, this will be the first time the carrier has flown from Christchurch since 2019.

The airline will face direct competition on the route, with Jetstar having already launched a seasonal service on the route recently with its Airbus A321LR. Air New Zealand will be the only airline from the South Island providing premium cabins on the route, with the service operated by the airline's Boeing 787-9. These flights will operate seasonally through May.

A nonstop flight from Christchurch to Perth takes just over seven hours gate-to-gate, while return flights surpass six hours. The return of nonstop flights to Western Australia is bound to be popular for travelers on both sides of the Tasman, and saves the unnecessary stops in either Auckland, Brisbane, or Melbourne for connecting flights.

Fleet resurgence is fueling bold nonstop links from Auckland and Christchurch, with Air New Zealand adding Western Sydney and Tokyo services.

On October 28, Air New Zealand resumed nonstop flights from Christchurch to Singapore. The last time the carrier operated on the city pair was in 2019, and through the airline's long-standing joint venture with Singapore Airlines, it provides seamless transfers through SQ's network at Changi (SIN).

Air New Zealand initially planned to operate the route seasonally; however, it has already announced that the route will now be operated year-round, complementing Singapore's year-round daily service to Christchurch, which increases to ten weekly across the Southern Hemisphere summer season.

Found an error? Send it info@simpleflying.com so it can be corrected.

This exciting expansion of international long-haul flights from Christchurch comes as the city has experienced rapid population growth. The airport is the second busiest in the country, and the only other airport outside of Auckland that is capable of receiving the Airbus A380, to which Emirates provides daily services with the double-decker on its Dubai - Sydney - Christchurch route.

Should You Buy Dutch Bros Stock Before the End of November?

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Does the calendar matter for Dutch Bros investors? Not really, but the price might.

I can't quite shake Dutch Bros (BROS -2.16%). The stock has spent most of 2026 sliding downhill. On Nov. 17, it sits near $35 a share, about 43% below where it started the year. Every time Dutch Bros' stock looks like a bargain, it finds a new floor to fall through. Meanwhile, the drive-through coffee shop chain keeps opening locations like it gets paid per ribbon-cutting.

So, should you buy before the end of November? Let me spare you the suspense: the calendar doesn't care. There's no earnings report due before the month runs out, and the next one isn't expected until February 2027. If you buy Dutch Bros stock, you should do it because of the price and the business, not because November is running out of days.

The third-quarter report gave Wall Street plenty to grumble about. Revenue jumped 30%, which sounds great until you lift the lid. Systemwide customer visits grew a whisper-thin 0.4%. Nearly all the same-shop sales growth came from people paying more per order, not from more cars pulling up to the window.

In other words, Dutch Bros' famous round-the-block car lines stopped growing longer in recent months.

Management also trimmed its full-year outlook. It now expects at least $2.1 billion in revenue and roughly $380 million in adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA), after calling for up to $2.13 billion and $390 million in August.

Here's what keeps me interested. Dutch Bros ended September with 1,273 shops and plans to open about 250 more in 2027. Management says it has already lined up the real estate for 2,029 shops by 2029.

I appreciate punny business goals, but it's also a downright ambitious target. Staying on track to meet it is an impressive feat, and Dutch Bros did it again in Q3 2026.

After the 2026 sell-off, Dutch Bros trades at about 30 times forward earnings. That's a steep multiple for a restaurant chain, but a more modest one for a company growing revenue near 30% a year.

If you have a long investing horizon and a sturdy stomach (more coffee might help), a 43% price drop for a business growing this fast deserves a closer look. November isn't a deadline for buying Dutch Bros stock, but it does look tempting right now.

Cillian Murphy's 28 Years Later: Part III Will Only Happen Under One Condition, Says Director Danny Boyle

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Are we going to see "28 Years Later: Part III?" Director Danny Boyle is certainly interested in doing it and Alex Garland has written a script. The problem? Money. This year's "28 Years Later: The Bone Temple" finally delivered the moment fans have been waiting for, bringing back Cillian Murphy as Jim. It was a small appearance teeing up something much larger in a presumed third installment. As Boyle recently explained, that movie is only going to happen if they can find the money.

Speaking recently with Deadline during an event for his new movie "Ink," Boyle's tabloid media origin story that will make you feel sick, the Oscar-winning director addressed the status of the third and presumably final entry in the "28" franchise. "There is a plan for a third part of '28 Years Later.' Alex has written a fantastic script," Boyle said. "We gotta raise the money to make it."

Sony Pictures produced both "28 Years Later" and "The Bone Temple." And while "28 Years Later" killed it at the box office, the sequel didn't. That's why Sony isn't eager to give it the green light. Speaking further, Boyle said that the third installment would bring the whole franchise to a close:

"[Cillian Murphy]'s up for it, and it's a fantastic part. Alex has written this fantastic part for him. Those of you who saw 'The Bone Temple,' the second part to '28 Years Later,' he's teased at the end of that. The third film does complete the story in a beautiful, mathematically perfect way, in which the circle is closed."

Creatively speaking, critics and audiences generally agreed that both of the recent "28" movies were damn good. But Hollywood is a business and money became an issue pretty quickly. "28 Years Later" made $151 million against a budget in the $60 million range. That was good enough. "28 Years Later: The Bone Temple" flopped at the box office though, bringing in just $58.5 million worldwide on a similar budget. Unfortunately, the math doesn't math for anyone who wants to make money in this business.

That's why "28 Years Later: Part III" probably won't happen. Admittedly, "probably" is doing some heavy lifting there. Cillian Murphy is an Oscar-winning actor for his work in "Oppenheimer," Danny Boyle is an Oscar-winning director, and Alex Garland is an Oscar-nominated screenwriter. It's a powerhouse team. That makes it unlikely but not impossible.

The most realistic path to getting it made would be partnering with a streaming service. Netflix is releasing Boyle's new movie "Ink" and the streamer also has a partnership with Sony. That would be the logical choice. But if Boyle and Garland want it as a more traditional theatrical release? That's where it gets more complicated and far less likely.

As for where it all went wrong? The bonkers ending in "28 Years Later" left audiences divided, for one. There was also a very short, roughly six-month gap between installments, which seemingly hurt more than it helped. But playing Monday morning quarterback doesn't help now. It's really about Boyle and co. getting creative to see if they can cobble together the money to bring this story to a close.

Economic Headwinds Drag Homebuilder Confidence to New Low

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Homebuilder confidence in November’s National Association of Home Builders (NAHB)/Wells Fargo Housing Market Index (HMI) report slumped four points to 25, a new 14- year low amid low consumer sentiment and significant pressure on the housing sector.

Last month’s homebuilder confidence score, a measly 29 points, was already a 14-year low. This month’s new slump sends homebuilder confidence even further back in time to April 2012.

NAHB blamed a variety of causes, the most important of which were “high mortgage rates, ongoing inflation pressure, elevated energy and material costs, (and) rising labor shortages and hesitant buyers.”

“Builders are confronting multiple challenges in today’s economy,” said NAHB Chairman Bill Owens in a statement. “Stepped-up immigration enforcement is keeping legal workers off job sites, deepening labor shortages and driving up costs. Higher fuel prices are prompting subcontractors to raise their rates sharply, and with fewer buyers in the market, more builders are scaling back construction.”

Robert Dietz, NAHB chief economist, focused on the housing market’s supply-side challenges, homing in on “building material costs pushed higher by tariffs, an ongoing shortage of construction workers and excessive regulations that undermine housing affordability.”

Dietz also added that “(m)ortgage rates above 7%, softer consumer confidence and an uncertain economic outlook are holding buyers back.”

The NAHB/Wells Fargo HMI, an ongoing monthly survey of more than 40 years, asks builders to rate their perceptions of current single-family home sales, potential single-family home sales in the next six months and prospective buyer traffic. Builders’ perceptions on each of these three component indices is scaled onto a range of zero to 100, which is then used to calculate a general homebuilder confidence level.

The three component indices this month were, unsurprisingly, also down. Current sales conditions dropped six points to 28, while future sales conditions dropped two points to 30.

Prospective buyer traffic ticked down two points to 16, another new low for this report. The last time prospective buyer traffic reached these troughs was 15 years ago (excluding a brief period at the start of the pandemic in 2020).

The NAHB/Wells Fargo HMI this month also reported that 46% of builders reduced prices, up 3% from last month. This is the highest share of builders cutting prices in the post-Covid period.

Almost seven-in-10 (68%) builders offered incentives, also up 3%, representing a post-Covid high. November 2026 is now the 20th consecutive month in which at least 60% of builders utilized sales incentives.

Regional HMI scores were similarly gloomy. All four regions experienced declines, but the Northeast and the Midwest experienced the largest declines point-wise at four and five points, respectively. The Northeast now sits at 34 points, the Midwest at 35, and the South and West both tumbled three points to 27 and 23 points, respectively.

Audrey Jiang is an editorial intern for RISMedia.

“A price cut tells you the seller’s first number did not work. It does not tell you what the home is worth,” says one real estate professional. “Some reductions create a legitimate opportunity. Others simply bring an unrealistic price closer to where it should have started.” Read more.

Scammers Tried to Trick Noah Kahan Fans With a Fake Show Outside an L.L. Bean Store

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Two New England icons will definitely not be teaming up this summer, with the clothing company L.L. Bean recently warning fans that a purported Noah Kahan concert outside its flagship store is a scam.

Last week, word started spreading online that Kahan was set to play a special pop-up gig outside the L.L. Bean flagship in Freeport, Maine on June 12, 2027. Tickets were even being sold on an Eventbrite page, which (per screenshots) billed the show as “Summer Camp Featuring Noah Kahan.”

A description read. “Summer Camp is here. Explore the pop-up, and when the sun goes down, the lawn opens for an evening concert with Noah Kahan.”

On Saturday, L.L Bean warned fans about the scam, writing on Facebook, “The listing has no affiliation with L.L. Bean and no such event is occurring at our Flagship campus. Once it was discovered we immediately notified Eventbrite and they have disabled the listing.”

It’s unclear how tickets were sold to the fake show, or how refunds would be handled. Eventbrite did not immediately return a request for comment. Kahan hasn’t spoken on the scam attempt either, and his reps did not immediately return a request for comment.

The concert industry has, unfortunately, long been rife with outright scams and sketchy practices. Typically problems arise around ticketing, especially on the secondary market, where phony tickets are often advertised on social media and sold through bogus websites. But fully making up a show appears to be a relatively new, if unfortunately inevitable, scammer escalation. Earlier this year, the Swedish guitarist Yngwie Malmsteen had to warn fans after someone started advertising and selling tickets to a bunch of fake gigs.  Trending Stories Watch Buffalo Springfield Come Together for the First Time in Many Years Jennifer Lopez Teases New Music on ‘Fallon’: ‘This Does Feel Like a Happy Era’ Cynthia Erivo, Travis Barker Honor Phil Collins With Big Vocals, Big Drums at Rock Hall Ceremony Priscilla Presley Scores Partial Win Over Fraud Suit, But Claims About Lisa Marie’s Death Can Stand

That someone would try the same scam with Kahan, one of the biggest artists around, feels especially bold. That said, this particular scam seems to have been kinda well thought out. The L.L. Bean flagship in Freeport does host an outdoor concert series during the summer, and Kahan has collaborated with the brand in the past. And while the flagship, as many Kahan fans noted online, is not as big as the stadiums Kahan is used to playing these days, the singer-songwriter has been known to do an underplay on special occasions.

So while the scammer may have been shrewd in concocting this part of their plan, there was one particularly glaring flaw: Kahan already has a real show scheduled on the same day.

He Cleaned O’Leary’s Boots at Leeds - Now Lewis Hamilton and Drake Buy His Clothes

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Leeds United reached the semi-finals of both the UEFA Cup and Champions League in successive seasons in 2000 and 2001, in what was one of the most successful moments in the club's history.

Leeds had players like Harry Kewell, Mark Viduka, Jonathan Woodgate, David Batty and Rio Ferdinand playing at the highest level, with the latter duo eventually earning moves to Real Madrid and Manchester United respectively in their careers.

The Whites were finally beaten by Valencia in the last four of Europe's elite club competition in 2001, having beaten AC Milan, Lazio and Deportivo La Coruna on their way to the semis.

The Yorkshire outfit also had many players coming through their academy system looking to become the next Alan Smith or Robbie Keane in the first team, with Aaron Lennon and James Milner two players who emerged into the first team in the years following.

Rav Matharu was in the age group with Aaron's older brother Anthony, signing a professional contract at 17 years of age, having been born in Leeds and then progressed through the ranks as a Leeds-mad boy.

There were inevitably extra eyes on him as a young Asian player, as both he and Harpal Singh reached the fringes of the first team at the same time. But he says he never got close to making a first-team appearance for Leeds, having also failed to appear for the reserve side, which often consisted of first-team players too.

Matharu said: "I don't think I got close. Maybe I'll tell people differently in the pub after a few drinks, but I don't think I was near to breaking through to the first team at Leeds.

There were internationals playing in the reserves, it was hard to progress. I remember being on the bench for the reserves against Sheffield Wednesday, and that was probably the closest. Chris Waddle was playing for them and I was watching him, remembering idolising him at the 1990 World Cup.

"It was hard to break into that team because it was doing so well. There were internationals in the reserves like Jason Wilcox and David Hopkin. It was a club where you had to do exceptionally well to even get on the bench of the first team."

After leaving Elland Road, Matharu admitted that he began to fall out of love with football after trials with other clubs, and after failing to find a suitable next home, he began to work in retail - and never looked back.

Speaking about his next steps, he said: "I always used to draw. I was into clothing and footwear and designing. It seemed like something I could do. I really wanted to learn more.

I went to Loughborough University to do a foundation degree in art and design, and was then offered a place at London College of Fashion. I went there for two weeks but dropped out because the expense was too much. I moved back to Leeds and worked in retail for three years, but I always felt I had to do something more with my life. So I picked up my studies at Leeds College of Art.

"I didn't know how to sew. I didn't know how to cut patterns. But I knew I had a good eye for fashion and clothing, and from a young age I'd always wanted to know how it was put together."

After moving to London to pursue a dream of working in fashion, he was let go from his role as Head Designer at House of Billiam, which forced him to set up his own business.

He added: "When I moved to London I always had the intention of starting my own brand. The name was born at uni; a lot of people cut patterns with scissors, but I would always use a scalpel, and my tutor would constantly tell me I was like a surgeon. I'd built a small website and registered the name clothsurgeon, but didn't really do anything with it until it was time to do my own thing."

Clothsurgeon has gone from strength to strength, with Matharu explaining exactly how the process works.

"We concentrate on quality fabrics, quality production. You can come in and create a wardrobe of quality clothes.

We offer a full bespoke service where you can make whatever you want, timeless silhouettes with a contemporary edge that can last you a lifetime. Beautiful overcoats that will not go out of fashion in your lifetime. A bomber jacket, a suit, whatever it is.

His clothes have become renowned for offering unique details, taking off massively since October 2012.

Since then, Matharu's clothes have been purchased by the likes of Kendrick Lamar, A$AP Rocky, Drake, Sir Lewis Hamilton, Ian Wright OBE, Kevin Hart and Eric Cantona.

The brand has also included collaborations with Coca-Cola, Nike and Selfridges, while he became Savile Row’s first bespoke streetwear store.and the first south Asian designer to open on the Row.

It's been quite the change of direction from cleaning O'Leary's boots at Leeds, but Matharu is thriving.

Leeds could pull off a huge signing in January - a deal which would be on par with their signing of Will Lankshear.

New-Home Buyers See Lower Rates as Builders Offer More Incentives

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A young couple surveys the construction of their new home.

Despite continuous woes in housing construction, homebuyers may see some deals on the table if they buy a new home vs. an existing home.

With affordability having become a large problem for the new-home industry, builders have been offering incentives, price cuts and more to buyers in order to complete sales on their homes.

In October’s existing-home sales report, NewHomeSource and Zonda Chief Economist Ali Wolf even said that, in her opinion, new homes continue to offer better options for consumers with more sales at lower prices, a trend that will likely continue in the new-home market through the end of the year, as builders work to meet their year-end goals.

“As we watch existing home sales next month, buyers will still see more options and incentives for new homes in the fall and winter, making it the prime season for new-home shopping,” she continued.

Now, a new report from Realtor.com® has found that buyers of new homes are also seeing reduced mortgage rates.

The report found that new-home buyers saw an average starting mortgage rate of 5.01% in Q3, compared with 6.15% for existing-home buyers. This translates to about $258 less per month in interest during the first year of homeownership (on a $400,000 home purchase with 10% down).

While some of these rate reductions are only temporary (via rate buydowns), the report also noted that the fixed-rate mortgage also saw a lower rate for new-home buyers. Specifically, new-home buyers saw an average rate of 6.19%, compared with 6.68% for existing-home buyers. This translates to about $117 less per month in interest for the 30-year loan term (on a $400,000 home purchase with 10% down).

“Builders have adjusted to a market where affordability is still a major hurdle, but that adjustment isn’t always visible in the listing price,” said Realtor.com Senior Economist Joel Berner. “Reduced mortgage rates can help make a new home more affordable for buyers while allowing builders to hold asking prices relatively steady.”

“A price cut tells you the seller’s first number did not work. It does not tell you what the home is worth,” says one real estate professional. “Some reductions create a legitimate opportunity. Others simply bring an unrealistic price closer to where it should have started.” Read more.

The NY Giants have multiple paths to the playoffs

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The New York Giants had the postseason clearly in their sights in the dysfunctional NFC East…until they let victory slip through their hands against the Washington Commanders last Thursday evening. Now they have made life more difficult for themselves. If you experienced the Tom Coughlin era, you'll probably remember that this is nothing new. Let's take a little trip down memory lane:

2007: In Week 14 the Giants beat the Eagles at The Linc 16-13 to get to 9-4 and put the playoffs clearly in their sights. The the next week they laid an egg at Giants Stadium, losing to Washington 22-10. Not until Ahmad Bradhsaw's 88-yard TD run as the snow started to fall late in the game in Buffalo the following week did the Giants clinch a playoff spot.

2011: In Week 14 the Giants won in Dallas 37-34 when Jason Pierre-Paul blocked the Cowboys' last second field goal attempt to get to 7-6. Next week, at home against Washington, the Giants were embarrassed by a 4-9 Washington team at home, 23-10. Not until Victor Cruz's 99-yard TD reception to spark a win against the Jets and a final week 31-14 win over the Cowboys for the division title did the Giants clinch a playoff spot.

It just seems to be in the Giants' DNA to play poorly against Washington. Now we're three years and running that the Giants haven't beaten them (coinciding with the arrival of Jayden Daniels). Anyway, that's in the rear-view mirror, and the question is whether the Giants can bounce back as they did in 2007 and 2011 to get to the playoffs anyway. No one sees the Giants as a Super Bowl contender (more on that in a minute), but as 2007 and 2011 showed, you never know. Unlike those seasons, the 2026 Giants are in the driver's seat in the NFC East even after their loss. Unfortunately, the wins have all been against losing teams and they now face a stretch of some of the best teams in the NFL. What are their chances of reaching the post-season, and which games have the most leverage on their chances?

To answer that we'll use The Athletic's NFL playoff simulator. The simulator gives the current chances of each team making the playoffs, as a division champion or wild card, and allows the user to see how individual games affect your team's chances. Let's play with the possibilities.

Let's get one thing out of the way right away: The Giants will not make the playoffs as a wild card this season:

The Giants' chances of winning the division and making the playoffs are identical, as they are for every NFC East and NFC South team. The Eagles are out of the picture. The Giants' chances to win the Super Bowl, however, are effectively nil at this point. How does each remaining game affect the Giants chances? Some matter more than others, but at this point they're all fairly important:

Obviously the Dallas game in Week 18 is their highest leverage game because it could directly decide the division champion. The differences among the other games are all about the NFL tiebreaking critieria. For a two-team tie these are:

Let's look at a few scenarios. Beating the Jaguars this Sunday would be big, increasing the Giants' playoff chances from 61% to 76% according to the simulator without considering any other games. A loss however drops their odds to almost 50-50. Fear not – a win the following week over the downtrodden Colts would get them back to two-in-three playoff odds. The figure below shows the odds with me having selected the coming two games in black:

Now let's consider the brutal reality that the 49ers and Seahawks are next on the schedule. Not that either team is unbeatable – the 49ers had their hands full with Dallas last Sunday and the Seahawks are having a post-Super Bowl hangover. Still, the Giants will be clear underdogs in both games barring injuries to multiple key players on either team. So assuming two more losses:

Three losses in the next four weeks, making it four of five defeats for the Giants, only reduces the playoff odds to about even. Why? It's because the Cowboys have their own gauntlet to run. After presumable gimmes against the Titans and Eagles in the coming two weeks, they face the Seahawks, Rams, and Jaguars after that, before playing the Giants and Commanders in their final two games.

And that's the key: Even if the Giants stink up the joint in the coming month, the odds are that they still face the Cowboys in Week 17 with the division title on the line.

So what is the Giants' most important game? Actually, it's coming up right after Jacksonville. The Giants absolutely must beat the Indianapolis Colts, who are in disarray after injuries, trading several of their best players, and Daniel Jones' carriage turning into a pumpkin in his second season as a Colt. If they don't, and they lose those other games as well:

Even then, all might not be lost if the Cowboys play to form because of their tough December schedule. Let's imagine that the Giants beat the surprising Cleveland Browns in Week 15, lose to a tough Detroit Lions team the week after, and then take care of business in Dallas and at home against the Eagles in the season finale:

In this desultory performance scenario the Giants weather a five-game losing streak, only win three more games, and still have a 72% chance to win the division at 8-8-1. That's almost as high as the 76% chance they'd get just by beating Jacksonville this Sunday. Beating the Jags and the Colts increases their chances to 85%, and that only drops to 77% if they lose to the 49ers and Seahawks as expected:

All in all, I would say that the second scenario I presented above (lose to JAX, SF, SEA, beat IND) is the most realistic outcome for the coming month. That means that there are decent odds that it's a coin flip by late December whether the Giants will make the playoffs, and it will all come down to that last month. It could have been so much better if they'd beaten Washington even once, never mind twice as could have happened.

Still, things could be worse. You could be a fan of a team that only won its first game of the season on Monday night, or a fan of the team they beat that jettisoned its former head coach and may watch him in the playoffs while they stay home. (By the way, if the Los Angeles Chargers run the table they have a 52% chance to sneak into the playoffs.) Talk is cheap, though. Beating Jacksonville would be worth more.

New Hyatt Credit Card Offer: Earn 4 Free Nights—Why I Spend On The Card Towards Elite Status

The World Of Hyatt Credit Card (See rates and fees) has a new initial bonus: earn 4 free nights at any Category 1-4 Hyatt hotel or resort after spending $5,000 on purchases in the first 3 months from account opening. That’s an improved offer from a card whose bonuses have usually been more modest than competing travel cards.

I have this card, and I put actual spending on it. Hyatt points are still valuable, but what gets me to use the card is the credit toward elite status and the rewards earned along the way. Hyatt status does more to improve my hotel stays than status with other major chains.

These are four separate certificates, so you can use them for one trip or spread them across different stays. You’ll need standard room award availability, and the certificates expire 12 months after they’re issued. The terms require checkout before the expiration date. Under Hyatt’s current award chart, a Category 4 standard room can cost as much as 25,000 points per night. Four certificates could therefore cover nights that would otherwise require 100,000 points. Their category restrictions and expiration date make them less flexible than having those points in your account. The card has a $95 annual fee. Chase excludes current holders of this card and previous cardmembers who received its initial bonus within the past 24 months. The usual Chase restriction on applicants who’ve opened five or more personal cards in the past 24 months also generally applies. Why I Keep The Card And Spend On It The annual Category 1-4 free night after each cardmember anniversary makes the $95 fee easy to justify. There’s also a second Category 1-4 night after $15,000 in purchases during a calendar year which makes that spending more rewarding. And these nights are transferable to another World of Hyatt member. If I won’t use one, I can give it to someone who will. For spending, the card earns 4 Hyatt points per dollar on qualifying Hyatt purchases; 2 on restaurants, airline tickets bought directly from airlines, local transit and commuting, and fitness club and gym memberships; and 1 on other purchases. More significantly, it comes with 5 qualifying nights each year, plus 2 more qualifying nights for every $5,000 spent. Those nights count toward both status and Milestone Rewards. Put $15,000 of otherwise unbonused spending on the card during a calendar year and you earn: 15,000 Hyatt points. An additional Category 1-4 free night certificate. 6 qualifying nights from spending, giving you 11 including the card’s annual 5. That means 49 qualifying nights actually spent in hotels, plus the card’s 5 annual nights and 6 from spending, gets you to the 60 needed for Globalist. And the $5,000 spent earning the initial bonus counts toward the $15,000 free-night threshold in the calendar year when you spend it. Milestone Rewards start at 20 qualifying nights and continue every 10 nights through 150. Some of the rewards on the way to Globalist are: Qualifying Nights Notable Rewards 30 A Category 1-4 free night, plus a choice of additional reward. 40 A Guest of Honor Award, plus a choice that includes a Suite Upgrade Award. 50 A choice that includes 2 Suite Upgrade Awards. 60 Globalist status, a Category 1-7 free night, 2 Suite Upgrade Awards, 2 Guest of Honor Awards, and My Hyatt Concierge. The value of another $5,000 in spending depends on where those two nights get me. Crossing a threshold for suite upgrades or Globalist can be worth giving up a higher earning rate on another card. Hyatt Status Is Stronger Than At Other Major Chains The card itself comes with Discoverist status. That’s better than a hole in the head as my grandfather used to say. But reaching Globalist has real value – as well as the milestone choices along the way. The biggest benefit is confirming a suite at the time of booking with a Suite Upgrade Award for up to seven nights when an eligible standard suite is available for the stay. That’s a huge value on family trips. I also value having a My Hyatt Concierge I can email requests to, actual delivery of 4 p.m. late checkout at eligible properties, and a more generous breakfast benefit than I get from other chains. At participating hotels without an available club lounge, Globalist breakfast generally covers a full breakfast for up to two adults and two children registered in the room, including tax and gratuity. Late checkout has exceptions, including availability limits at resorts, but I’m usually proactively offered late checkout everywhere else (and personally find it honored more often than not at resorts). The Free Night Certificates Should Be More Flexible The current version of this card launched in 2018. Its annual certificate is still capped at Category 4, while category creep has taken away many of the hotels where I’d most want to use it. Just this year, 14 hotels moved from Category 4 to Category 5, including Hyatt Regency Seattle, Hyatt Regency Grand Cypress and Hyatt Regency Jersey City. Five moved the other way, bringing options like Hyatt Centric Congress Avenue Austin and Dream Nashville within reach, but the certificate lost more hotels than it gained. I’d like Hyatt to at least let members add points to a certificate to book a higher category. The certificate works at a Category 4 hotel charging 25,000 points, but won’t work at a Category 5 hotel when it’s charging 15,000 points. And I wish Hyatt would let us combine these free nights with Suite Upgrade Awards or Guest of Honor Awards. I value them most traveling with my family when I’m paying for rooms out of pocket. That’s always when I want to use my suite upgrades! I don’t need a suite on a business stay (paid by someone else, when it’s just me) or to use the free night award. The Best Fishing Stocking Stuffers for Anglers Who Have Everything

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

For bass anglers chasing the latest trend, the Rapala CrushCity CEO urchin bait is a strong pick. For versatile hard-bait options, the Z-Man ChatterBait Elite EVO HD and Rapala Shad Rap cover different conditions and species. For practical gifts, the Boomerang SNIP line cutter and Rapala Curved Pliers are tools any angler will actually use on the water.

We may earn revenue from the products available on this page and participate in affiliate programs. Learn more

Shopping for an angler can be tough, especially if they already have a tackle box full of gear. The good news? There are plenty of smaller gifts they’ll get excited about, from new-for-2026 baits and clever fishing gadgets to a portable speaker built for long days on the water. We rounded up some of our favorite fishing stocking stuffers that are fun to unwrap and, more importantly, will be useful long after the holidays.

Urchin baits are the hot new trend in bass fishing this year, and the Rapala CrushCity CEO is one of the most popular. Its tentacled design creates plenty of movement in the water, while the weighted core helps it sink without adding extra weight.

Our experts dubbed the Z-Man ChatterBait Elite EVO HD one of the best chatterbaits this year, thanks to the improved hook and vibrating hex-shaped blade. It comes in a bunch of different blade colors and skirt patterns, so there’s one to fit almost any angler’s needs.

The Rapala Shad Rap is a classic crankbait that belongs in any bass or walleye angler’s tackle box. Its tight wiggle mimics a fleeing baitfish, while its versatile design works well in both shallow and deeper water.

They’ll be grateful for these snips the next time they’re out on the water. The handy cutter quickly and cleanly cuts through any type of fishing line—mono, braid, and fluoro—and has a carabiner so your giftee can clip it right to their vest or pack.

From removing hooks to trimming line, a good pair of pliers earns its place in any tackle box. Rapala’s Curved Fishing Pliers have a curved nose for reaching hooks in tight spots, plus side cutters and a spring-loaded design for easy one-handed use.

According to our bass fishing expert, the Berkley PowerBait MaxScent worm is one of the best smallmouth lures of all time. Its soft body has a natural action in the water, and the scent helps convince finicky fish to hang on a little longer.

Plano StowAway boxes have been a tackle-box staple for generations, and the 3400 is a handy size for keeping hooks, weights, and other small fishing essentials organized. Its adjustable compartments let you customize the layout to fit your gear.

For early morning launches and late nights on the water, the Black Diamond Spot headlamp makes it easy to see what you’re doing without fumbling around in the dark. It puts out up to 400 lumens, and the red light mode helps preserve your night vision when you don’t need the full beam.

You can never go wrong with a new hat, especially for someone who spends most of their free time on the water. Marsh Wear has become a favorite among anglers, and the new Heritage Hat stands out with its bass fishing print and classic trucker style.

Bring some tunes along for the ride with the popular JBL Clip 5. This compact speaker clips onto your gear bag or boat, is waterproof and dustproof, and delivers up to 12 hours of playtime (or 15 with Playtime Boost) for long days on the water.

We’re big fans of the Yeti Rambler lineup, and this 10-ounce stackable mug is one of our favorites. It has a DuraSip ceramic lining for a smooth drinking surface and double-wall vacuum insulation to keep coffee hot even on the chilliest mornings at the boat ramp.

The Gear Aid HeroClip Mini is one of those little gadgets you don’t realize you need until you have one. It combines a carabiner with a swiveling hook, so you can hang a gear bag off the ground or clip essentials to a backpack, keeping your stuff off wet decks and muddy ground.

The Fishpond Shallow MagPad Fly Puck is a handy little gift for fly anglers. Its magnetic base keeps flies secure between casts, so you can swap patterns without losing track of your go-to options.

For the angler who has everything, the Simms Fish Whistle 2.0 is a fun little addition to their fishing kit. It clips to a pack or gear bag and doubles as a whistle, giving you a quick way to signal a fishing buddy when you’re spread out on the water.

For more than 125 years, Field & Stream has been providing readers with honest and authentic coverage of outdoor gear. Our writers and editors eat, sleep, and breathe the outdoors, and that passion comes through in our product reviews. You can count on F&S to keep you up to date on the best new gear. And when we write about a product—whether it’s a bass lure or a backpack—we cover the good and the bad, so you know exactly what to expect before you decide to make a purchase.

Experience Amanda taught herself how to shoot a bow when she was bored one day—and a new obsession was born. Since then, she’s fallen in love with bowhunting and later waterfowl hunting (thanks to her husband who took her on her first duck hunt in 2020!). Amanda came to Field & Stream in August 2022 from USA Today with nearly a decade of commerce and product testing experience. She’s passionate about the outdoors and spends as much time as she can shooting her bow, hunting with her husband at Hart-Miller Island, and hiking with their German shepherd. It’s lightweight and portable, heats up in seconds, and has saved me on many a frigid duck hunting mornings when we’re in the jon boat or blind. Notable Work

Star Wars’ Thrawn Creator Is Bringing a Murder Mystery to Star Trek

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Hannah covers horror, sci-fi, and games at Collider, where she writes news and features, reviews new releases, and interviews the people who make them. She’s helping build out Collider’s gaming coverage, with Pokémon as one of her specialties. If she isn’t writing about a monster, she’s probably watching one on a tape from her VHS collection.

Timothy Zahn built one of Star Wars’ most enduring villains around the pleasure of watching someone notice what everybody else has missed. Thrawn can make a conversation about culture feel like the beginning of a military disaster, especially when the people across the table haven’t realized how much they’ve already told him. That makes his creator an intriguing person to put in charge of a murder mystery.

Zahn introduced Thrawn in Heir to the Empire in 1991, giving Luke, Han, and Leia a formidable new opponent after Return of the Jedi. Thirty-five years later, he’s taking that interest in secrets and competing agendas to another corner of science fiction. His destination is Deep Space Nine, a station where a valuable object changing hands is usually enough to get several people interested in who’s buying, who’s selling, and what neither side wants anyone else to know.

Zahn is writing Star Trek: Roc’s Egg, an original graphic novel releasing from IDW Publishing in October 2027, with art by Ángel Hernández. The story begins when an artifact arrives aboard Deep Space Nine and appears to be destroyed in transit, and a Federation JAG investigation follows, uncovering a secret auction, wealthy collectors, dangerous criminals, and a murder waiting to happen. Hernández’s previous Trek work includes Voyager — Homecoming and Deep Space Nine — The Dog of War.

Zahn’s relationship with Star Trek actually predates his work in Star Wars. In IDW’s announcement, he traced it back to watching the original series in 1966, describing the opportunity to work on the graphic novel now as “a huge honor and privilege,” making this a chance to contribute to a franchise he’s followed since its beginning.

The station is a promising place to start. A mystery aboard a starship puts suspects among people who generally have a reason to trust one another. On Deep Space Nine, the investigators have to contend with visitors whose business may already depend on keeping Starfleet at a distance. Add a private auction, and an apparently destroyed artifact becomes a much more interesting problem. For readers who know Zahn through Thrawn, having multiple angles to look at this story from is the draw. His most famous character wins by understanding what other people value and how far they’ll go to protect it, while Roc’s Egg gives him a station full of people with something to hide, and an investigation that could make those secrets expensive.

Star Trek: Roc’s Egg releases in October 2027.

Samantha Morton on Being a Woman in Hollywood: ‘We Are Here as a Family’

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Costume designer Ellen Mirojnick delivered the actress’s moving remarks on her behalf at our event.

Yesterday evening, The Odyssey star Samantha Morton was unable to attend ELLE’s 2026 Women in Hollywood celebration because she was sick with the flu. But the honoree had the film’s costume designer Ellen Mirojnick deliver her moving remarks on her behalf. Morton reflected on the honor of playing Circe in The Odyssey—and the greater lesson she has learned about what being a woman in Hollywood means, decades into her acting career.

She wrote, “As women in this industry, we know what we do isn’t easy. We love it, we are passionate about it, and we are fortunate to do it and it’s great when it all comes together. But we know this isn’t always the case, and there can be many obstacles and pitfalls along the way in an ever-changing and sometimes very cruel business.

“I keep asking myself what it means to be a ‘Woman in Hollywood,’” Morton continued. “It was a childhood dream for me to be here. To act in films that would travel the world and be remembered. For almost 30 years, I have visited and worked here, and for a time, I called this city my home. Every year, I returned to the same house in Beechwood Canyon, a house built by Dolores del Río, the first Mexican movie star, yet I never fully felt that I belonged or that I had ‘made it.’

“Tonight shows me that to truly be a woman in Hollywood is not about location but community. We are here as a family; a community of creatives, and we must support each other as we fight for our right to work in a safe environment. To be treated fairly and respectfully. To keep pushing for fair and equal pay and parity for female actors and filmmakers. We will get there.”

Watch Morton’s full speech above and read her Women in Hollywood cover story here.

“We're not saying they're perfect” says architect fighting to save Melbourne's public housing blocks

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Photography by Ben Hosking, Michael Pham

Architect Simon Robinson has been leading calls for the Victorian state government to rethink its plan to demolish all of Melbourne’s public housing towers. In this interview, he sets out why he believes retrofit is the smarter approach.

Announced by the Labor-run state government in 2023, Homes Victoria’s High-Rise Redevelopment Program (HRRP) will see all 44 of Melbourne’s public housing towers and many other lower-rise blocks razed and replaced with new housing by 2051.

Officials argue that the buildings, constructed between 1962 and 1975, have reached the end of their useful lives. They claim the towers do not meet modern standards and cost too much to maintain, and that the redevelopment programme will triple housing capacity and improve the quality of homes.

But the policy has proved highly controversial, with critics particularly angry about a lack of transparency or consultation with residents, who are being relocated.

It is now subject to an ongoing legal dispute, has prompted concern from the UN’s special rapporteur on housing, and become a political issue in the build-up to the Victorian state election later this month.

Among the voices leading calls for a change of course is Melbourne not-for-profit research and design practice Office.

Office has worked pro bono to make the case for refurbishing the towers, after learning from residents that the government has not published feasibility studies for keeping any of the condemned estates.

“We thought that was pretty odd that you wouldn’t do a refurbishment study on these estates,” Office director Simon Robinson told Dezeen.

“So we took it upon ourselves to start to do these feasibility studies, along with public housing residents.”

“We’re not saying that they’re in perfect condition,” he added. “We’re also not saying that all of them should be refurbished – perhaps some are beyond repair. All we are saying is that these rapprochement studies need to occur.”

In fact, feasibility studies have been conducted by the government but not been made public, despite requests by the Victorian Legislative Council.

By avoiding the relocation of residents, Office estimates that a retrofit and infill approach would cost around $300 million less than demolition and rebuild.

“As all the estates are occupied, the cost of relocating and housing the residents during construction work needs to be included in the project cost; this is consistently excluded and never disclosed,” said Robinson.

“We have estimated that these costs are very significant, with reports indicating that the government is spot purchasing or head-leasing properties from the private market.”

The practice has put together a counter-proposal based on a strategy that it calls Retain Repair Reinvest (RRR).

RRR focuses on retaining buildings without displacing residents, carrying out renovation work to improve housing comfort and energy efficiency while reducing construction waste and carbon impact.

Robinson agrees that most of Victoria’s existing public housing buildings are currently not fit for purpose – buildings are rundown, cold in winter, and sweltering in summer. But he believes comfortable living standards can be achieved through renovations.

Office has applied its RRR strategy to three Melbourne estates in hypothetical case studies – Ascot Vale Estate, Barak Beacon Estate and the Flemington Estate.

Formerly home to approximately 10,000 people, Flemington Estate’s four towers make up about 10 per cent of Victoria’s public housing stock.

Only about 32 households remain, though an injunction prevents Homes Victoria from serving further eviction notices while legal challenges are pending.

Office has proposed retaining the estate’s 720 dwellings and building an additional 577 infill social housing units to increase density.

New facades and private balconies would be added, with other upgrades including integrated underground parking, a central communal green space, new heating and cooling systems, improved insulation, new kitchens and bathrooms.

Energy efficiency is another key focus. Double-glazed windows, photovoltaic cells, and water conservation systems would provide a 55 percent reduction of global warming potential compared to the demolition approach, according to Office.

Despite the seemingly obvious benefits, the Victorian government has so far been unmoved by the studies presented by the practice.

Robinson believes the reason lies in the fact that the state government no longer wants to manage public housing.

“When the state government manages public housing, they are responsible for maintaining the buildings and organising new tenants – all the admin that comes with owning a building,” he said.

As well as the demolitions, also attracting concern is the type of housing proposed for redevelopment under the HRRP.

Rather than like-for-like public housing, the new homes will be “community housing” – developed by private developers, and split between below-market and market-rate rental homes.

Click here to check out our Social Housing Revival series.

Despite still being classed as social housing by the state government, community housing is slightly more expensive, and the tenancy is less secure from eviction.

Robinson believes the term was created to misdirect the public.

“What they’re building is community housing, not public housing, so there’s no longer any public housing being rebuilt in Victoria,” he said.

“The government is now calling everything social housing, because they don’t want the public to know that it’s no longer public housing,” he continued.

“Victoria has the lowest percentage of public housing stock in Australia, and we’re currently demolishing it all and turning it into private and community housing.”

The issue is particularly charged in the context of Australia‘s housing-affordability crisis. There are currently over 60,000 people on the waitlist for social housing in Victoria, where social housing accounts for three per cent of all dwellings, below the national average of 4.2 per cent and the OECD average of 7 per cent.

“Public housing serves as foundational social infrastructure that treats shelter as an essential human right,” said Robinson.

“By offering rents permanently capped to income and delivering unmatched security of tenure, it provides an unconditional safety net for society’s most vulnerable households.”

Office’s work has gained political attention. In November 2023, the Victorian Legislative Council debated the merits of the RRR approach, with the council’s Legal and Social Issues Committee urging the government to reconsider demolition and explore all refurbishment options.

The team can already claim a small victory. At Ascot Valley Estate, the first RRR case study released by Office, the development was quietly removed from the planned demolitions list on the Homes Victoria website and part of the estate has since been refurbished.

The estate is a collection of 57 three-storey brick housing blocks, built in 1947 and designed by renowned modernist architect and public housing advocate Best Overend during his time with the Housing Commission.

This gave Robinson and his team hope that the government would consider the alternatives if presented with the option.

“We have presented to Homes Victoria, we’ve presented to different ministers as well, and we’ve kind of heard that they are starting to look at retrofit some of their existing building stock,” said Robinson.

“It kind of suggests that perhaps they’re gonna prefer we show all of them. So we released the following studies on Barak Beacon Estate and the Flemington Estate.”

However, Barak Beacon Estate has since been demolished, and the construction of the redevelopment is due to complete by the end of the year.

Now the fate of Flemington Tower awaits a ruling from the High Court of Australia following a hearing last month.

A class action filed by Inner Melbourne Community Legal on behalf of public housing residents in North Melbourne and Flemington is on its last appeal, after the Supreme Court of Victoria and Victorian Court of Appeal ruled in favour of demolition.

Meanwhile, the Liberal opposition has pledged to pause the HRRP if it wins office at the upcoming state election.

Robinson hopes he and his team have done enough, and continue to raise awareness about the issue.

“We’ve gotten to a point where we feel we can’t just keep putting out these reports,” he said.

“The papers we produced are quite dense and not for the general public. So what we’re now doing is translating that work into a series of publications, putting it in bookshops, and making it more accessible for the general public.”

“We continue interviews with different residents, different advocates, different professionals, speaking with journalists and doing lectures. The idea is to bring awareness to what’s occurring, rather than us doing more reports.”

He is also calling for a frank discussion in the architecture community about the redevelopment projects, which many of Australia’s prominent studios are working on.

“All the big-name architects are working on these estate renewals, some have even won awards as exemplar projects for these architects to promote their practice in terms of doing public good,” said Robinson.

“It’s really hard to find anyone who’s willing to talk about why they’re doing it, or is it good, the displacement and environmental issues. We’re alone.”

Dezeen put Robinson’s claims to the Victorian state government, but was told it could not respond in the lead-up to the election.

Photography by Ben Hosking, Michael Pham

More than just a football club: Cardinale’s vision to make AC Milan a multi-billion empire

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In case you weren’t aware from his repeatedly bold comments in the media, Gerry Cardinale has big plans for AC Milan.

The RedBird Capital founder and chief has made one concept very clear: while he is at the helm, Milan are no longer just a football club, but a platform that aims to integrate sports, infrastructure, and entertainment. Of course AC Milan will be at the centre of the web, but the idea is to expand.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The proof of this comes from a three-letter acronym: MSE. It stands for Milan Sports & Entertainment and it is the new holding company created by RedBird. The idea, simply put, is to bring together investments and activities in various sectors under one umbrella.

As La Gazzetta dello Sport recall, Cardinale explained to the Financial Times that the assets involved could be valued in the mid-single-digit billions, meaning several billion dollars. So, how does he plan to get there?

Cardinale linked the creation of MSE to the process initiated with the acquisition of Milan: “Milan Sports & Entertainment is the culmination of this work and of what we have built since we first invested in the club four years ago.”

In his interview with La Gazzetta, Cardinale clarified that the new structure is also created to support the Rossoneri’s sporting ambitions: “The driving force behind operations like this is to provide Milan with the financial strength needed to compete in the transfer market and secure the best players in the world.”

There are examples of this kind of model working already. Kroenke Sports & Entertainment, AEG and Fenway Sports Group are among the most prominent and – given his background in the USA – Cardinale knows each of them well.

According to CNBC valuations, KSE was worth $26.05bn in 2026, FSG $15.37bn, and AEG $11.99bn in 2025, excluding music and hotel businesses.

KSE boasts franchises such as the Los Angeles Rams, Denver Nuggets, Colorado Avalanche and Arsenal, as well as venues such as SoFi Stadium, Ball Arena, and Emirates Stadium. The group combines sports, infrastructure and real estate development such as Hollywood Park, the district built around SoFi Stadium.

AEG – founded by Philip Anschutz – have instead built its model on the management and development of venues. It owns or manages venues such as Crypto.com Arena and The O2 in London plus the Los Angeles Kings and LA Galaxy, and operates in concert promotion and ticketing. The LA LIVE project developed around Crypto.com Arena puts the venue at the centre of a greater ecosystem.

Fenway Sports Group – born around the Boston Red Sox and Liverpool FC – have developed a holding company with interests in media, marketing, real estate, and entertainment. It’s a model Cardinale knows first hand: in 2021, RedBird invested approximately $750m in FSG, acquiring a stake of around 11%, based on an enterprise valuation of $7.35bn.

MSE in theory can harness elements of all three of these experiences: KSE’s integration of sports and infrastructure, AEG ‘s commercial development of facilities and FSG ‘s multi-sport approach. The main difference is the purpose declared by Cardinale: to use all of it to funnel money back into Milan.

From the stadium shared with Inter to the possibility of an arena in San Donato, including the new NBA Europe venture and even tennis: MSE’s scope includes various initiatives that go beyond the playing field.

The main infrastructure project is the future Generali Stadium, the new 71,500-seat facility designed by Foster + Partners and Manica. Developed jointly by the two Milanese clubs, it represents a key element of the transformation of the San Siro area and will not be a Rossoneri-only facility.

The promoters intend for the stadium to host year-round activities, from matches to concerts, as well as catering, events, and hospitality services. In other words, like the stadiums and arenas mentioned above, it will become more than just a place to go for two hours on a Sunday.

In San Donato Milanese, RedBird are also considering the possibility of building a 20,000-seat arena. Cardinale explained that the fund already owns the land and has planning permission, but the idea is also linked to Milan’s potential involvement in NBA Europe. Another area of ​​focus concerns tennis, with the possibility of hosting a tournament in the city.

Finally, RedBird intend to develop a company dedicated to managing stadiums and arenas, with services including catering, merchandising and premium hospitality. The goal is to build commercial expertise to apply to the facilities connected to the group, but also to make it available to other owners and sports clubs.

This phase will be co-ordinated by Brett Yormark, appointed CEO of MSE and set to step down as head of the Big 12 (a conference in College Football) at the end of January 2027. He worked for 14 years at Brooklyn Sports & Entertainment, overseeing the development of the Brooklyn Nets and the Barclays Center. He has also gained experience in NASCAR and Roc Nation.

The choice to go for Yormark reflects the desire to entrust the platform to someone with expertise across multiple different fields in the sporting and commercial worlds.

All these grand plans are well and good, but how will ideas turn into money that drives AC Milan forward? MSE’s economic potential is measured not only through the club’s revenues, but also through the activities that RedBird intends to develop around sports, infrastructure and entertainment.

The starting point is obviously Milan as a club, which closed the 2025-26 financial year with revenues of €464.6m, over €100m of which came from sponsorships. The main infrastructure opportunity is the new San Siro, an investment of approximately €2.3bn shared with Inter.

Projections contained in the design documents indicate a potential annual revenue of approximately €120.4m for the facility as a whole (both for the Rossoneri and the Nerazzurri), through sporting, commercial, and complementary services

These are numbers that will be revised upwards by the two clubs when considering, for example, the record agreement with Generali for naming rights. It is worth a total of €650m over 25 years, divided into €10m per year for the first five years and €30m per season for the following 20, once the stadium is built.

One of the areas on which RedBird Capital are focusing most is hospitality, which is currently worth around €15m per year for the Rossoneri. The international benchmark has already been set by Legends Global, a company Cardinale knows well.

Legends are what the Milan owner sees as a model for developing premium services, restaurants, and commercial spaces. The most obvious example is at FC Barcelona: with the support of LG, the club have marketed nearly 5,000 VIP seats at Camp Nou, generating over €380m in contracted revenue.

They aim to generate another €700m through multi-year licenses for an additional 2,000 seats. This isn’t annual revenue, but rather contracts spread over 15 or 30 years, so a guaranteed flow of money coming in that Cardinale aims to replicate.

Another agreement which demonstrates the potential of this kind of arrangement is the one with Real Madrid for the Santiago Bernabéu. It involves an investment of approximately €360m in exchange for 20-year participation in some of the stadium’s businesses,.

Legends Global also operate in facilities and events management. Following the acquisition of ASM Global (who actually proposed renovating San Siro for non-football events in Italy) in 2024, the group claims a presence in over 450 venues, approximately 20,000 events, and 165 million guests annually. Their European partnerships include Arsenal, Chelsea and Porto.

FC Barcelona launches new VIP seating licenses at Spotify Camp Nou with Legends Global

🔗 More details: https://t.co/awtEsnYE6X pic.twitter.com/iCMuM0QD35

— FC Barcelona (@FCBarcelona) September 25, 2026

To illustrate the potential it could hold for Italy as a whole, the English subsidiary reported revenues of £131.6m in 2024, of which £105.5m came from facility and event management and £26.1m from catering. Before the integration with ASM Global, Legends had reported revenues of $1.7bn in the period ending in March 2024, with 67% coming from food and beverage sales.

In Italy, a benchmark is Forumnet, who in 2024 generated €29.7m in revenue and €12.8m in EBITDA (Earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortisation). They manage the Assago Forum which hosts approximately 60-70 concerts a year, demonstrating how a venue can generate activity well beyond the sports calendar.

As mentioned, an arena is being planned in San Donato with an estimated investment of around €400m, which could become the basis for a Milanese franchise in NBA Europe. For reference, Olimpia Milano – the current basketball team in the city – generate around €15m through ticketing, season tickets, sponsorships and merchandising.

For NBA Europe, projections speak of over $1.5bn to be distributed to the clubs in the first six years. A theoretical division among the 12 permanent franchises would equate to approximately $20.8m per team per year, but this is not fixed yet. Entry costs – estimated by the media to be between $500m and over $1bn – also remain to be determined.

Tennis represents another potential area for development. The ATP 500 in Halle generates around €5m in revenue, excluding television rights, while the ATP Finals in Turin generate around €60m. These are events with different dimensions and scopes, but they provide a scale.

The creation of MSE has a stated objective: to strengthen Milan’s financial capacity and reduce the gap with the richest clubs on the international scene, particularly those in the Premier League.

The gap is also evident when comparing revenues. AC Milan closed the 2025-26 season with €464.6m, a significant result considering their absence from European competitions , but still far from the top English clubs.

According to the Deloitte Football Money League, in the 2024-25 season, Liverpool generated €836.1m, Arsenal €821.7m and Manchester United €793.1m. Tottenham and Chelsea also exceeded €670m and €580m, respectively.

The comparison covers different financial years, but it still shows the magnitude of the gap. Liverpool generated revenues equal to approximately 1.8 times those of Milan, while Chelsea recorded a turnover approximately €120 higher.

The chasm shows how clubs in the Premier League have showcased an ability to generate commercial revenue, enhance stadiums and reach a global audience. The gap has only been growing, and TV rights will be another focus, though that is outside of just Cardinale’s hands.

Speaking of the owner, it is now obvious he sees MSE as the name that will house the tools to try build a real sporting and commercial world around Milan. It remains to be seen how effective it will be, but the starting point is there.

Tiller Review – Stop Typing Transactions Into Your Budget Spreadsheet

GSK won U.S. approval for its second lung cancer drug from the Nuvalent deal

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

The FDA granted accelerated approval to neladalkib, sold as Nelkarmi, for ALK-positive non-small cell lung cancer

GSK won accelerated approval from the U.S. Food and Drug Administration on Tuesday for neladalkib, a lung cancer drug sold as Nelkarmi.

The FDA cleared the kinase inhibitor for adults with locally advanced or metastatic anaplastic lymphoma kinase (ALK)-positive non-small cell lung cancer. Eligible patients have received one prior ALK tyrosine kinase inhibitor consisting of alectinib, or two or more prior ALK inhibitors and platinum-based chemotherapy, the agency said.

GSK gained neladalkib through its $10.6 billion acquisition of Nuvalent, according to Bloomberg. The deal closed in July, according to Reuters.

The acquisition also brought Jideytro, which the FDA approved on July 22 for adults with locally advanced or metastatic ROS1-positive non-small cell lung cancer who have received at least one prior ROS1 inhibitor. Nelkarmi is the second of the two lung cancer drugs from the deal to win approval, according to Reuters.

The FDA granted the approval on an accelerated basis because an early-stage trial found notable anti-cancer effects and a safety profile resembling that of current ALK-targeting drugs, according to Bloomberg. A later-phase trial meant to establish how much the drug ultimately helps patients is still ongoing, Bloomberg said.

The approval gives patients who have run through the available targeted therapies another option, according to Reuters. For those patients, the principal fallback has frequently been chemotherapy.

"I think we're making great progress with the oncology portfolio," Hesham Abdullah, GSK's head of oncology R&D, said in a briefing, according to Bloomberg. He said GSK is assembling a franchise of lung cancer drugs, and that it has additional medicines in development for blood and gastrointestinal cancers.

GSK reported higher third-quarter sales and core operating profit last month, with oncology sales up 23% to £600 million ($786 million). CEO Luke Miels cited Jideytro among three new U.S. specialty medicine approvals in recent months, alongside Hibsago for chronic hepatitis B and Jemperli for rectal cancer.

Oncology is a business GSK exited a decade ago, according to Reuters. Miels is leading its return to the field, and the Nuvalent purchase marks the boldest step GSK has taken so far toward re-entering oncology.
